## 2026-11-14 — Scope review hunk reads to the current file

## 2026-11-09 — Distinguish modifier-only review from direct keyboard shortcuts

Option-held Shift-release review entry uses `MacReviewKeyMonitor` and `KeyboardReviewGesture`; legacy Option+Shift+Next/Previous arrives through VS Code keybindings. The latter working does not establish that the separate key-state sampler can see modifiers. During this report, the same 1.2.164 host had entered eyes earlier that day, while the current Carbon `IsSecureEventInputEnabled()` check returned true and the OS named the Codex app as owner. The executable name “ChatGPT” alone is misleading: check its bundle identity. Apple [TN2150](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/technotes/tn2150/_index.html) explains the cross-process keyboard blocking boundary. Secure Input was an initial diagnostic lead, before the physical probe and recovery recorded above. Check OS state before random reinstalls or gesture timing changes, and never bypass another app’s keyboard protection. No runtime behavior had changed at that initial diagnostic checkpoint; the subsequent patch is recorded above.

Follow-up physical probe: Ethan's Option-held Shift taps changed `CGEventSourceFlagsState` modifier flags, while `CGEventSourceKeyState` for those same four modifier positions stayed all-up for both combined-session and HID-system sources. Secure Input remained enabled. A normal Extension Host restart activated the same release afresh, but Ethan confirmed the gesture still failed. This establishes unavailable key-state reads during real input, not a recovered gesture or proof of the exact cause; release of Secure Input by its owner has not been tested. A long-running `NSWorkspace` foreground query without a notification run loop may be stale and must not be used as focus evidence.

The installed VS Code 1.145 workbench also fires `onDidChangeWindowState` for `active` changes, not only `focused`. Its `$onDidChangeWindowActive` and `$onDidChangeWindowFocus` both call `onDidChangeWindowProperty`, which fires the same event. At this checkpoint, `MacReviewKeyMonitor.focus()` unconditionally stopped/restarted its reader on either event, so idle activity could discard a pending gesture. Opus identified this separate source defect, subsequently patched as recorded above. It does not by itself explain the sustained physical failure above. Preserve that distinction rather than shipping an idle-race fix as proof the reported failure is solved.

## 2026-11-05 — Staged Changes expansion: native defaults versus Better Git triggers

Ethan reported Staged Changes open on a fully staged `eslint.config.ts` (Index) review and asked that it never open unless he opens it. Excluding that staged file from the hold is correct. No mouse trace exists, so the exact first expansion is unproven. Read-only inspection of the installed VS Code 1.144 workbench established the possible causes:

- SCMViewPane's tree uses `collapseByDefault: !(repository || resourceGroup || resourceFolder)`, so every newly created group is expanded. Groups always report children. An empty Staged group is hidden by a tree filter (`resources.length > 0 || !hideWhenEmpty`); Git sets `hideWhenEmpty = !git.alwaysShowStagedChangesResourceGroup`. A repository's groups are omitted when all are empty. Refreshing a parent recursively discards a collapsed child's descendants, and reopening it recreates them through the default. A new repository, a root reopened after a full-tree refresh (any repository opening or closing), or a clean→dirty worktree can therefore show Staged expanded without any extension command.
- Auto Reveal (`scm.autoReveal`) resolves the active editor's primary URI, scans groups last→first, then calls `expandTo`, setSelection and setFocus. It runs on active-editor changes, when Source Control becomes visible, on visible-repository changes and on view-mode changes. A `git:` Index side never matches a row; a staged-only `file:` editor expands Staged.
- The Worktree link's `collapseStagedGroup` skips a hidden empty Staged group, which cannot receive focus. A fresh Codex worktree therefore reveals Staged expanded on its first stage. The existing native harness already covers the always-visible empty-group configuration (`empty-staged-no-input`).
- Better Git-owned trigger: after staging a worktree's final unstaged file, its retained working view was staged-only `file:`. Any later trigger above expanded Staged and selected that row. `presentStagedOnlyReview` now replaces only that clean, still-active view of a file the same command staged with its Index review, in the same worktree and editor group. The same guard covers Stage without advance; the automatic conversion bypasses opt-in staged-row reveal, preserves focus and maps the current cursor/viewport to the identical Index bytes. Partially staged files, unrelated/dirty tabs and presentation failures retain the old view; stage receipts and Undo are unchanged. `revealStagedInSourceControl` (default off) deliberately expands Staged and remains opt-in.

There is no public per-group expansion API or setting. The qualified session scroll uses a phase-bearing pixel gesture, not a phase-free wheel detent: Agentic Mouse's existing gesture pass-through must not count presentation as another file-selection ratchet. ## 2026-11-03 — Worktree focus logs do not establish review intent

The retained normal-workspace log from 30 October through 3 November 16:23 UTC contained 68 received Worktree requests: 60 completed, 31 with native presentation pending in the background and 29 without it; eight reported incomplete. Those are presentation states, not evidence of reading or of Codex being the foreground app. Never turn that split into a review-versus-Codex preference statistic.

Explicit URI links now have bounded local debug observations, with unique click IDs and one outcome. Exclude the scripted origin/editor handoff; fast focus races and passive reading remain unknown. Reuse the existing debug LogOutputChannel and retention instead of another store. No paths or contents enter these usage events, no polling or input delay is added, and diagnostic failures cannot break commands. The source-checkout summary deduplicates IDs and retains unknown, pending and missing-click buckets.

Ethan subsequently chose to return to Codex again “for now”, because that feels more useful. His existing User settings now retain worktreeLinkReturnFocus=true and worktreeLinkReturnApp=com.openai.codex, with worktreeLinkKeepEditorFront=false. This is a private local choice, not a new public default and not measured user behavior. Configuration is read for each link, so this setting change does not need a host restart.

# Learnings

## 2026-11-06 — Startup selection needs a current snapshot, not a new selection event

Agent Flow startup can follow a range highlighted before its watcher was attached. The companion must distinguish an explicit `captureKind=startup` request from live gesture capture. Startup reads the currently selected single visible editor range directly, or the stable local terminal selection tool for an explicitly proven terminal surface. Echo the purpose, surface, nonce and fresh observation time; do not require a recent selection-change event for startup. Keep live event freshness, source-PID/window validation and the 8,192 UTF-16 limit. This adds no model request, Copy, clipboard read, focus command, selected-text cache or polling.

Version1.2.162 passed251 non-GUI checks, TypeScript, lint and production packaging on the Mini. Source and packaged integration exercised real editor, diff and terminal highlights made before a startup request, rejected empty/multiple selections and preserved fixture files. The exact authenticated/public Marketplace archive gate passed for VSIX SHA25699a16a8da7ec72cafb1c4a56fe33e661a890438bc896cc367f5d3bbd042ef98a. Gallery installation alone did not activate162; the Better Git owner's subsequent163 release retained the same two bridge sources and supplied activation. Its live process-bound163 receipt and private selection socket identify host67111 under Code7855, with Gallery UUID, unpinned status and source preserved. Native Agent Flow489 separately waits for initial Code capture before automatic HUD typing focus; the isolated non-activating HUD probe had shown why Code's frontmost PID alone was insufficient. Physical acceptance remains separate.

## 2026-10-31 — Native Worktree folding commands can finish after their command promise

Ethan reported both Staged Changes and other worktrees remaining expanded after a Worktree link. The live 1.2.141 log at 15:26:51 UTC loaded aimvs39, deferred native presentation until focus, then returned loaded=false at 15:26:56 while the link generation remained current. Those records establish an aborted presentation, but do not identify the precise guard or prove one timing cause for every incident. Keep per-command debug records, explicit expiry/cancellation reasons and ownership diagnostics rather than treating every missing completion as slowness.

Opus inspected the installed native workbench implementation: resource-group focus queues work without returning that operation's promise; asynchronous tree expansion has the same readiness problem. A disposable Mini driver delayed the actual native group-focus operation by 350 ms while returning immediately, reproducing a wrong final selection with the 1.2.141 bundle. Version 1.2.142 uses one 500 ms settling constant after explicit-link tree expansion and group focus, including deleted-file Changes-group selection. This is a bounded native presentation gap, not a review-input debounce or a guarantee under arbitrarily long renderer stalls. The optional origin-to-Code handoff also waits for acknowledgement of the requested Code activation; it does not repeatedly activate an app or steal focus back from another app.

Preserve the existing global repository-minimize command, exact selected-repository check, one recent-link retry and ten-second deadline measured from the click. Review input cancels the initial foreground pass as well as an already-deferred pass. A throwaway background-without-focus experiment passed with SCM previously focused but failed with Graph previously focused, leaving Staged expanded; do not remove the focus guard. Background tests must await the accepted presentation's actual completion before reading its final rows: file selection happens earlier and sampling that intermediate state is a test-oracle error. The test must never repair the tree or issue another collapse.

These 111 native checks include repeated common cases across configurations, not 111 distinct physical user journeys. The first six runs injected 350 ms fire-and-forget native group focus; the app-order run used actual native activation without that injected delay. ## 2026-10-31 — Resume unfinished text reviews without retaining editors or blocking input

Ethan wants a visited unstaged file to resume its last cursor, selection and scroll position when returning through SCM or Better Git navigation. Keep session-only coordinates keyed by the full file URI, bounded to 512 recent files and 16 selections per file; never retain file contents or an editor per cached file. Observe the active tab's actual text side, including read-only deleted text after live unstaged membership is verified. Images retain their separately agreed zoom/pan reset behavior. Successful Better Git staging and native Git state changes clear completed positions; failures keep them. The three-entry exact Undo history remains independent and its captured view, like explicit gesture cancellation, takes precedence.

Capture the target's saved view before opening it. Native first-hunk landing can create a new coordinate entry during open; mistaking that entry for an earlier visit suppresses first-visit backward EOF/last-hunk landing. Native SCM revisits use one identity-guarded event-loop callback after the open event batch, cancelled by a newer tab or real cursor move. Return-position presentation does not wait on viewport settling, Git, polling or a new input queue; Undo keeps its existing settling behavior.

The production bundle `1c32e5b54a9cb69205168c597bdddcafa4866f1668358e45017263ed3787bf60` passed all 195 non-GUI checks, TypeScript/lint/packaging and 50 focused Mini-host checks (host exit zero). New fixture failures were corrected before qualification: AtTop setup did not account for editor context padding, and an actual held index.lock makes native Git retry rather than returning the intended API failure. Use the suite's existing isolated add-failure injection for that check; successful staging and Undo still use real Git.

## 2026-10-31 — Copy Path labels are not canonical filesystem paths

The live 13:05 log rejected a deleted screenshot as `/~/Projects/...` and then refused keyboard review because no change was captured. The same working-tree path was absent from HEAD but present in the index as a 130-byte LFS pointer. These are independent facts: the path error prevented Git membership; a missing working file or HEAD blob must not make an unstaged deletion ineligible. The clipboard fallback itself was unchanged since 80835fc (2026-08-29), so this evidence does not establish that the recent keyboard mode change introduced the bug.

VS Code's Copy Path command uses its label service even for absolute paths; an opaque media tab can yield `~/Projects/...`. Passing that label straight to `Uri.file` produces `/~/Projects/...`. Expand only the home-label prefix at that one clipboard boundary. Keep the raw clipboard result for ownership-safe restoration; known public tab resources still avoid the clipboard, exact Git membership remains mandatory, and no disk-existence guard, repository guess, added delay or input cutoff is introduced.

## 2026-10-30 — Qualify Mini native control before relying on OpenClaw setup

The Mini was unlocked, and its earlier SSH/CDP editor tests remained a different route from native desktop control. Screen Sharing asks for authentication to its own connection; opening that dialog does not establish a Mini sign-in requirement. The alternate installed Peekaboo controller also reported neither Screen Recording nor Accessibility permission, so it was not a working native fallback.

OpenClaw's `/codex computer-use install` is an owner/operator-admin management command. Its ordinary CLI `chat.send` requests a narrower scope by default; the legitimate configured CLI helper with explicit `operator.admin` scopes admitted the command without forging sender identity. A `started` acknowledgement or successful `agent.wait` result only proves command processing; inspect the matching fresh command response. While a command is pending, `chat.history` can still end with the previous attempt's result.

The Mini had native control disabled, an explicit `features.plugins=false` startup override and a marketplace folder where the installer expected a file. Removing those conflicts exposed a reserved-marketplace refusal in the standalone backend. OpenClaw's supported managed desktop route subsequently installed native MCP tools, but its actual `mcpServer/tool/call` readiness calls timed out twice. No screenshot, AX result or Code Accessibility grant was obtained. Do not rename a reserved marketplace, spoof native caller identity or claim tool inventory establishes working control.

Enabling native setup also caused ordinary Mini agent turns to fail before model start because provisioning refused an unowned bundled-marketplace cache. Preserve that ownership safeguard. After qualification failed, the four changed runtime fields were restored through the hash-guarded config API; a fresh read verified the exact runtime revision was applied, the original fields were restored and every other configuration value was unchanged. The existing Mini agent then executed a normal turn, read its session status and replied over Agent Bridge, confirming ordinary recovery only. Leave native setup and SCM lookahead unqualified until real native access works; keep the accepted Better Git installation intact.

## 2026-10-29 — Source Control lookahead needs viewport access

Ethan wants the current batch-review file moved near the top of the native Changes list when too few following rows remain visible, leaving roughly ten files below it. The installed workbench's SCM Auto Reveal calls `tree.reveal(resource)` without a relative position. The extension API exposes neither that built-in tree nor its viewport. The built-in `list.scrollUp` / `list.scrollDown` commands move only ten pixels on the last-focused list; they do not accept a resource or a row-at-top position. Do not approximate this by walking selection through other files or issuing blind scroll commands against a possibly focused Graph.

Native inspection identifies the main list as AXOutline “Source Control Management”; visible resource descendants include full worktree paths in their descriptions. Match the exact resource, rather than assuming the SCM selection equals the Better Git preview: the isolated keyboard fixture had c.txt open and eyes-marked while the native row selection still pointed to a.txt. macOS Accessibility access must be checked in the responsible application context. Both the Mini's SSH probe and its Code-owned terminal probe reported no access; the MacBook's existing system permission record grants Code Accessibility. A Mini failure therefore does not justify asking for another MacBook grant.

No scrolling implementation is qualified or installed from this investigation. The isolated viewport planner and presentation queue have ten passing non-GUI checks; the native adapter and integration remain unfinished. The earlier keyboard fixture was used for intrusive terminal/permission probes, after which its pending review was cancelled; that resumed fixture is not a clean staging regression result. Preserve the previously qualified 1.2.139 behavior while completing a separate scrolling test.

Follow-up setup evidence separates three different boundaries: the Mini already has an ethansk console session, the prepared Screen Sharing connection asks for its own authentication, and the Code-owned Accessibility probe remains false. Read-only TCC inspection shows the Mini's official Codex CUAService already has Accessibility and ScreenCapture permission. OpenClaw's generic NodeREPL route failed because it lacked `nodeRepl.createElicitation`; its isolated official unified-runtime provisioning then found the older Mini ChatGPT bundle lacked `@oai/cua-repl/bin/cua-repl.mjs`. Neither failure establishes that the Mini is offline or that another MacBook permission grant is needed. Do not simulate elicitation or edit TCC to work around a controller/runtime incompatibility.

The installed Mini bundle also contains the original official `computer-use/bin/computer-use-client-launcher mcp` connector. A real SSH stdio MCP initialize/tools-list probe succeeded against that original launcher, exposing native app-state, click, scroll and key tools. Check this compatible existing connector before concluding that the desktop app needs an update. Tool discovery alone does not establish working UI control or a Code permission grant. The unnecessary Screen Sharing login dialog was dismissed; the prepared newer app copy was discarded without activation.

The Mini's `IOConsoleLocked=false` readback confirms it was unlocked. Direct app inspection first failed with `procNotFound` or timeouts; a subsequent independently verified GUI-session broker (its own `id -A` reported the GUI audit session) reached an explicit `Sender process is not authenticated` refusal. That is native caller authentication, not evidence that Ethan must sign into the Mini. Do not spoof a sender or disable approval checks. Restore the supported local OpenClaw/native-host integration and require a real screenshot/AX result before claiming access works. The task-only broker, socket and LaunchAgent were removed; Code Accessibility was not changed by these probes.

## 2026-10-29 — Option-held Shift release alternates keyboard review modes

Ethan corrected the keyboard contract after 1.2.138: hold Option, tap/release Shift to enter Review changes (eyes), then alternate eyes and Stage files (explosion) without an empty mode while holding. Preserve the same populated selection across mode changes. Releasing every relevant held modifier/navigation key commits once; Shift taps never commit. Eyes excludes the current endpoint, file mode includes it. Escape and app blur cancel. Option+Shift+Next/Previous retains immediate whole-file staging and its original physical repeat guard; that chord consumes its Shift tap, so its release cannot also change mode or commit an old batch. Mouse/wand transports and lifecycle remain unchanged.

VS Code offers no modifier-only key-up event. The focused-window MacReviewKeyMonitor uses the established read-only CoreGraphics key-state query (macKeyReleaseMonitor.ts), with one owned JXA process and a 10 ms native sample, not an extension-host timer, event tap or input injection. It checks four modifier positions, the four existing navigation positions during a gesture, and only a keydown count to disqualify ordinary Option+Shift+arrow/text shortcuts. The child stops on blur/disposal; parent death and malformed output fail safely. No permission prompt, per-ratchet process or navigation debounce is added. The pure KeyboardReviewGesture interpreter owns mode and release rules; shared hold selection/transaction/Undo own files and index changes. This supersedes the 1.2.138 instruction below against adding a release observer: final release is now explicitly requested.

The native workbench script also passed its baseline worktree cases, real legacy renderer shortcut, final release, eyes exclusion, Escape and exact Undo. ## Wand range reversal and actual extension activation

A wand has no physical Next/Previous button defining a permanent selection direction. In a live trace, a session started Previous, then forward file-boundary crossings kept just one file because the fixed-direction range treated them as undo steps. Give only the wand an endpoint-rebase option in the existing MouseStageSelection helper: reverse steps still remove the departing file, but once only one endpoint remains, either direction can begin collecting again. Physical mouse holds retain their prior fixed-direction policy. Both file and eyes navigation continue through the same existing transaction and exact Undo code.

Installed files are not proof of activation. Running Extensions showed 1.2.129 after the Gallery had installed 1.2.130 and Restart Extension Host had run. Confirm the actual Running Extensions version after the supported extension update/reload action; otherwise the Eyes setter can remain absent from the active runtime even though the installed bundle contains it. The wand's explicit finish action stages its set; app focus loss must cancel its pending set rather than borrowing physical mouse auto-stage-on-switch.

## 2026-10-18 — Reduce snapshot subprocesses without changing history retention

History baseline order is retention metadata: do not simply skip its no-change writes while claiming identical behavior at the 100-root boundary. The conservative optimization reads commit identity and tree together with an explicitly formatted, unsigned, undecorated `git log -1 --no-walk ... HEAD --`. It keeps every fresh index-tree read, queue position, shared lock, history write/retention policy and fallback to the original rev-parse flow. There is no snapshot cache, dropped event, input debounce or staging delay. A live replacement object must affect the next read even when HEAD object identity is unchanged.

## 2026-10-18 — Distinguish remaining pre-stage wait from redundant observer writes

After 1.2.128 activation, normal-workspace stage-flow logs at 13:39:29 BST showed 954 ms waiting for observer preparation, 218 ms for Git add and 214 ms to open the next review. Five following operations prepared in 0 ms; their Git adds took 195–236 ms. Their next review opened before or independently of receipt completion, confirming deferred navigation on the installed runtime without treating one good sequence as full performance acceptance. Git's own output recorded 68 status completions in 13:39 (median 858.5 ms) and 26 in 13:40 (median 150 ms); these are Git status events, not proof that Better Git initiated them all or that any one event caused the observer wait.

A disposable non-GUI store probe confirmed another source-level inefficiency: ten alternating notifications for two repositories with identical snapshots caused ten history writes and zero Undo entries. `setBaseline` moves each observed repository to the end of the bounded baseline list; `update` sees that order change and rewrites the file. This is not a measured fraction of the user's lag. Avoid removing exact snapshots or reordering accepted operations merely to reduce writes; retention order and cross-window safety must be explicitly tested if optimizing it.

The old host also reported a TypeScript 5.9.3 completion assertion through `MylesMurphy.prettify-ts` and native Git decoration capping. These establish separate errors/load, not causation for stage latency. The display-mode probe runs every five seconds and on focus, but it is independent of the stage command and this audit did not prove it blocks navigation. No runtime code, settings or user workspace were changed in this follow-up audit.

## 2026-10-18 — Stage-and-next waits behind shared Undo observations

Normal 1.2.127 logs show real 5–14-second stage-and-next delays. At 13:08:40 BST, preparing the Undo snapshot took 6067 ms, Git add took 353 ms, and saving its receipt took 7574 ms; total 13997 ms. The live Undo JSON is 29205 bytes with three receipts and 100 baseline records, so an unbounded Undo-entry buffer is not supported by this evidence. Do not add navigation delays or remove exact cross-window Undo checks in response. Any performance fix must preserve accepted operation order and detect real index/HEAD changes; same visible file/status labels alone are not a safe cache key. ## Magic Wand review uses the existing staging transaction

The wand is an independent review source, never a Corsair/Razer alias. Its strict versioned URI
lifecycle carries an owner UUID, monotonic sequence, direction and change/file mode. Queue gaps
before release; a missing packet must not commit an incomplete range. Duplicate/retired owners
stay inert, active physical-mouse holds reject wand admission, and lost heartbeats cancel the
wand's own transaction. Change review calls the existing preview navigation and enables
Eyes mode; release excludes the current preview. File mode calls the existing file-range action.
All index mutation and exact Undo remain in the existing Better Git transaction.

## 2026-10-05 — Native foreground Worktree selection and cached Staged expansion

This supersedes the earlier draft's assumption that recursively collapsing an already-collapsed repository always clears its children's expansion state. A native focus trace proved that the intended repository header owned `list.collapseAllToFocus`, yet its cached Staged Changes group reappeared expanded when the root reopened. Expand only the validated target, focus Git's visible Staged group (optional Merge precedes it), collapse that group explicitly, then return focus to the repository header before previewing its top unstaged file. Do not recollapse later or cycle repository commit inputs.

VS Code 1.139.1 Git's `getOpenRepository(Uri)` sorts its own open-repository array in place by descending root-path length. Native SCM discovery order stays unchanged. A newly opened `fresh-staged` worktree consequently appeared first in the API but last in SCM, causing a counted position to select the wrong root. Retain root URI order from the existing open-repository observer, remove closed roots, and use workspace display names for configured name sorting. This adds no repository-status watcher or timer. The initial API snapshot can still be affected by earlier external sorting; hidden non-Git providers and persisted custom order are not proven equivalent. Position remains a hint and requires native `ui.selected` validation before group operations.

Real foreground Mini checks pass repeated switches, deleted first files, merge/conflict editor modes and eight clean/staged-only variants, including newly opened staged and clean roots. Sixteen extra alternating switches passed with focus tracing, without replacing the extension's focus predicate. The final production bundle also passed the 32-repository `--link-only` harness (331 staged / 395 unstaged entries) and single-implicit-repository variants with hidden commit input, hidden action button, clean state and an explicitly visible empty Staged group. Native screenshots confirm Staged stays collapsed. No MacBook E2E or Extension Development Host was used.

The native global repository-collapse command throws when a single repository has no header. Skip that missing-node command in this case, clear retained file focus, and begin from the first commit control or first resource group when both controls are hidden. Collapse Staged directly in either configuration; do not infer the starting group from retained focus.

All 33 focused Worktree-link integration cases, 129 non-GUI tests, TypeScript and ESLint pass. Image tests must close their disposable previews before resetting/removing the underlying fixture image: a delayed native media-editor disposal during the next link otherwise changes the active tab and correctly trips the existing ownership guard. This is test cleanup, not a reason to weaken user-navigation cancellation. The exact tested production JavaScript SHA-256 is `eb28f04bf5aa33de777bbf74efdbd1f3d501ef0bf1db33c8ef3968b82ad827e4`.

## 2026-10-05 — Bound explicit Worktree-link presentation; foreground acceptance pending

The 1.2.119 deleted-first fallback cycles `focusNextInput` across repositories. Live logs showed a failed deferred pass followed by another link attempt, and Ethan reported visible intermediate repository expansion and worse lag. This supersedes the recommendation immediately below to reuse that input-cycling fallback. Also avoid a quiet-period wait subscribed to every unrelated repository: a link refreshes and presents only its requested repository.

On VS Code 1.139.1, `list.focusDown` accepts a numeric count. After the native global `collapseAllRepositories` command and SCM focus settle, a single counted jump followed by `list.select` selects a collapsed repository header and updates the Git API's `ui.selected`. Contrary to the older July observation, this current native command does dispatch the repository selection. `list.collapseAllToFocus` recursively closes only the selected repository, then `list.expand` reopens its header with Staged Changes closed. Short waits are required between native queued steps; there is no repository-input loop and no pre-opening of an unrelated preview. Git API repository wrappers are recreated between API calls, so compare root URIs rather than JavaScript object identity.

Repository position is only a hint: hidden providers or a persisted custom order can differ from the configured Git order. Verify native `ui.selected` before operating resource groups; on mismatch close the candidate and stop, never cycle repositories or claim native completion. Explicit name sorting with reversed Git discovery order passed in a 32-repository diagnostic; hidden providers and stored order overrides remain an acceptance concern. A background link still opens the correct editor and defers native focus work; an actual user tab change cancels presentation ownership.

Draft verification: 129 non-GUI tests, TypeScript and ESLint passed. All 33 focused Worktree-link integration cases passed in the isolated Mini host. A separate diagnostic drove the actual compiled extension and native renderer with explicitly emulated DOM/window focus because macOS authentication remained frontmost. It passed with both 9 repositories in discovery order and 32 repositories using name sort after reversed discovery: new-worktree discovery with 331 staged / 395 unstaged entries, switches, repeats, deleted-first, clean, staged-only, newest-link-wins, exact repository expansion, Staged Changes collapse and unchanged Git/editor invariants. Its deleted-first, clean and staged-only screenshots were inspected. Emulated focus does not establish OS activation, real foreground timing, physical Chrome link acceptance or manual UI completion. This draft is not published or installed; finish foreground acceptance before releasing it.

## 2026-10-05 — Retain interrupted Worktree presentation and handle deleted first entries

The 1.2.118 deferred scheduler removed its focus listener before its queued asynchronous presentation completed. If the window lost focus while status/SCM settling ran, the native pass deferred again without reporting that result to the scheduler, leaving the link intent stranded. Keep the listener until asynchronous completion, retain an explicit deferred result and admit one later pass after an actual focus event. Focus regained while the pass is queued is remembered; no passes overlap, no timer polls, and cancellation/newer generations still invalidate admitted work. After admission, a known review URI owns the retry across Better Git's own preview input changes; never adopt a different manually opened file.

Include an explicitly visible empty Staged Changes group when computing that position. Preserve deleted content, the index, peer status and every workspace folder; do not create a temporary working file or walk SCM resource rows. Internal link logs now distinguish loading from native presentation still pending.

## 2026-10-03 — Performance audit: per-file Git reads and derived badge membership

Opus 5.5 independently traced an additional avoidable clipboard path. `currentReviewFileUriAsync` falls back whenever its direct resolver returns nothing and any active tab exists, including known clean text and Settings tabs. Parent verification executed the extracted current resolver/helper against mocked VS Code APIs: each such case made three clipboard reads, two writes and one Copy Path command before returning no review file. A known-input guard prototype made none, with the same result and sentinel; an opaque changed-image case retained its lookup and result. This is a mocked control-flow probe, not native timing. A future guard must preserve undefined/unknown image comparisons, stale-tab checks and clipboard ownership restoration, then pass the existing Mini image/navigation cases.

The claim in `getModifiedSideHunkGeometry` that VS Code's Git API has no per-path working-tree-versus-index string method is incorrect. Despite its name, `diffWithHEAD(path)` runs `git diff -- <path>`, without a HEAD argument, in VS Code 1.83.0 (Better Git's declared minimum), current upstream and the installed 1.139.1 Git extension. It therefore excludes already-staged edits just like the existing full `repo.diff(false)` followed by `extractFileDiffSection`. Check the actual implementation, not the method name or the older source comment. Primary sources: https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/1.83.0/extensions/git/src/git.ts#L1171-L1181 and https://github.com/microsoft/vscode/blob/main/extensions/git/src/git.ts. A future narrow optimization can use this existing API, preserve the staged path and retain best-effort failure behavior; it does not require a long-lived diff cache or a separate Git executable.

A disposable 300-file fixture with 160 modified lines per file produced 5,510,700 bytes for the full diff and 18,369 for one file. Three alternating warm pairs on the busy MacBook measured full diff 396.13/227.93/477.75 ms versus per-path 24.05/18.87/42.86 ms (medians 396.13 and 24.05). After staging that file and editing one further line, its per-path index-to-working diff was 640 bytes, matching the full diff's section, while HEAD-to-working was 18,370 bytes. These are synthetic subprocess measurements, not live VS Code latency or proof of the user's intermittent lag. The fixture was removed; private scripts and minimal receipts remain at `/private/tmp/bgv-performance-review-20261003`.

The batch decoration provider calls `selectedMouseStageItems` inside each row lookup and again for its tooltip count. That helper constructs a Set and filters the captured file list on every call; one render pass across a large selected batch repeats the same work quadratically. A Node probe using the compiled production helper asserted identical badge membership/counts for existing repeated calculation versus once-derived membership/count. Three 500-file passes measured 38.97/33.99/22.94 ms versus 0.12/0.17/0.10 ms; 1,500-file passes measured 521.46/391.53/318.00 ms versus 1.18/0.83/0.93 ms. This models a complete row-request pass, not the renderer's actual visible-row count. A future optimization should derive presentation data per immutable selection value, keep the eyes endpoint's different badge/count semantics, and leave release membership, reverse removal, request ownership and Undo untouched.

Do not optimize away the serialized Git observer, shared history lock, fresh index checks or accepted input order merely because they have a cost. Working-tree/status events can leave path/status labels unchanged while a partial-index tree changes; matching visible Git rows is not sufficient to skip an Undo observation. The three-entry history bound and Undo's `read-tree -m -i` stat-cache preservation remain present in 1.2.117. This was an investigation; no production source, settings, mouse mapping, package version or installed runtime changed, and no MacBook E2E/Extension Development Host was run.

## 2026-10-03 — One directional pending set survives every eyes/file toggle

Ethan replaced the origin-distance exception with one unconditional rule: moving opposite the original Next/Previous hold direction removes the departing file, in either eyes or ordinary file mode, including past the starting file. The former range plus preview union could resurrect a previewed file after toggling; discard both caches and keep one selected-index set, its current endpoint and the original hold direction. Forward file transitions add source and destination; reverse transitions remove source and add destination. Mode toggles never change membership, within-file hunk steps leave it unchanged and outer worktree boundaries clamp without writing Git. The set is bounded by the captured unstaged file list; no timer, debounce or traversal history is needed.

Ordinary file-wheel inputs had a separate release race: they checked the current active hold only when their queued work ran, so a release arriving behind accepted ratchets could drop those steps. Capture and admit the request on input arrival, then update its existing request-keyed released selection at the original queue position. Preserve cancellation, source ownership, foreground checks and immediate badge clearing. Release still performs one exact batch transaction with one Undo receipt.

## 2026-10-02 — Load Worktree links without an operating-system foreground gate

The reported “VS Code did not come to the front” message came from Better Git's own eight-second focus gate, before repository loading. Remove that gate rather than increasing it. An exact Worktree URI can validate/open its repository, refresh Git and open the top unstaged file while its receiving window remains behind another app. Clean and staged-only worktrees must preserve the current editor and index.

Native Source Control input/list focus is a separate presentation constraint. In a newly launched, unfocused VS Code 1.139.1 renderer, the Repositories pane could be visibly focused in the DOM while list navigation commands did nothing. Commit-input cycling repeatedly selected its prior owner. Extra waits, reasserting the pane and walking repository rows did not solve the cold background case; those experimental fallbacks were removed. Do not describe successful Git/editor loading as proof that all native groups folded in the background.

Keep one pending presentation per receiving extension host and complete it on the next actual window-focus event, without polling or a timeout. A newer link replaces it; manual cursor edits, document edits and Better Git navigation/staging cancel it. Keep cancellation effective after the callback enters the serialized link queue, not just while the focus listener is registered. Preserve the existing optional origin-app return and final Code focus sequence; a routine app handoff is not user review navigation. Foreground links retain Git/Source Control settling, recursive group collapse, peer-header collapse and native target reveal.

Test evidence: 122 non-GUI tests include focus-registration races, replacement, shutdown and queued-retry cancellation. The Mini's disposable VS Code 1.139.1 host checks exact outside-worktree paths, aliases, repeated links, last-file release behavior, conflicts, image inputs and clean/staged-only editor preservation. The cold-background native harness deliberately sends no CDP mouse/key events before its links: an earlier Graph click initialized renderer list focus while the operating-system window still reported unfocused, masking the real cold-start limitation. Native fixtures include nine worktrees and a busy target with 331 staged and 395 unstaged entries. The Mini authentication UI remains untouched; physical focus-transition acceptance is separate from background-load and scheduler proofs. No MacBook E2E or Extension Development Host was run.

## 2026-10-02 — Global unchanged-line visibility differs from per-file expansion

VS Code 1.139.1 has a native persistent setting, `diffEditor.hideUnchangedRegions.enabled`. `diffEditor.toggleCollapseUnchangedRegions` changes that setting, whereas Command Palette **Show All Unchanged Regions** and **Collapse All Unchanged Regions** change only the focused diff model. With global hiding enabled, showing all in one file does not disable hiding for the next file. The diff model also serializes its individual collapsed ranges, so different files can retain different expansion states while hiding remains enabled. Do not describe those per-file commands as global toggles or diagnose this distinction as a timing bug without a separate reproduction.

Disabling the setting removes hidden regions from ordinary diff views; their stored ranges cannot override an effective false setting. Ethan asked to restore unchanged code, so the user setting was changed to false with a complete backup and all other settings text preserved. Read-only native inspection confirmed continuous unchanged lines in his current ordinary side-by-side diff. No hiding override was found in the checked user-profile, untitled-workspace and project settings locations. No application restart or MacBook Extension Development Host was used.

The installed VS Code source has two limits relevant to a future Better Git toggle. The native toggle infers the configuration target from existing overrides, so it may change Workspace rather than User settings; an explicitly global extension command should choose `ConfigurationTarget.Global` and detect overriding scopes. The multi-file diff model forces `hideUnchangedRegions.enabled` to true, so changing the ordinary setting alone is not proof that this separate view will follow it. Avoid walking or reopening every file merely to implement the native ordinary-diff switch.

Ethan proposed holding the Stage owner (Corsair 8) and pressing cell 11 for a display toggle. Current Agentic Mouse source maps 11 to Switch App by default and Go to Definition in its entered VS Code mode; any future chord must intercept those actions at the input owner. Define the Stage owner's release outcome before implementing it. Do not reuse Better Git's existing adjacent cancellation blindly: `resolveAdjacentMouseChord` performs the previous exact Undo before readiness, and only cancellation after readiness. Button-down ordinarily captures the origin without navigating; immediate navigation is behind `experimentalMouseHoldNavigateOnButtonDown`, disabled by default. A source-tagged display URI must not decide whether an earlier or later keyboard release stages.

Evidence: installed VS Code commit `04c0d99f4fb0d8afe6ce4f0c58e31e183ac3e4b1`; `src/vs/editor/browser/widget/diffEditor/commands.ts`, `diffEditorViewModel.ts`, `src/vs/editor/browser/widget/multiDiffEditor/multiDiffEditorViewModel.ts`, and `src/vs/workbench/services/configuration/browser/configurationService.ts`. Source behavior and the current ordinary view were verified; a future mouse chord and multi-file workaround were not implemented or tested.

## 2026-10-01 — Resume change review from the visible viewport

VS Code scrolling leaves the editor selection behind, even when the caret remains on screen. Starting Next or Previous from that selection made Better Git jump relative to an old review position. The first press in an editor now anchors to its rendered viewport; subsequent presses keep the existing exact caret-owned progression until a separate visible-range change signals that review has scrolled. A weak map stores only the viewport's top and bottom lines and a scroll flag. Navigation operations suppress their own visible-range events, so rapid repeated presses retain their order and precise steps without polling, Git reads or an input delay.

## 2026-09-30 — Stage at each held preview wheel file boundary

With the Stage button and adjacent preview button held, the Agentic Mouse wheel sends Better Git's existing `navigate-mouse-stage-preview` command. Previously that command only navigated: crossing the final change in a file opened the next file, and the marked group was staged only on release. It now stages the marked group and reviewed file when a ratchet crosses that file boundary, then keeps the same hold stage-ready on the next unstaged file. A ratchet within a modified file still only moves between hunks. At the last unstaged file, the boundary stages it once and release cannot stage it again. Each boundary is its own exact Undo transaction; the existing three-entry Undo cap still applies.

Git's `getRepository(uri)` can return distinct wrapper objects for files in the same repository. Comparing those objects made every boundary stage silently fail; compare their `rootUri` values instead. Save the pre-ratchet editor view for Undo because VS Code's native change command can wrap the caret before the boundary is detected. A text diff without an attached visible editor is not ready to establish a file boundary.

## 2026-09-29 — Use the existing display probe for optional VS Code zoom

The opt-in `inlineDiffOnDisplay` watcher already identifies the chosen external display by UUID or unambiguous size. Reuse its five-second probe for `zoomLevelOnDisplay`, rather than adding another watcher. A complete pair of finite VS Code zoom levels from -8 to 8 is required; an empty or invalid pair leaves zoom alone. Apply the selected level on startup, display transition or preference change, then remember that display/configuration state so a manual zoom is not overwritten by the next poll or window-focus check. An ambiguous display match leaves both diff view and zoom unchanged. The setting updates VS Code's `window.zoomLevel` globally and affects the application, not only Better Git's Source Control view.

Ethan's saved VS Code `window.zoomLevel` is `-1`, while the last active normal Code window's persisted `uiState.zoomLevel` is `1`; the latter matches its current window bounds. His `coffee_shop_coding` OBS profile points to recordings on the mounted SD card. Four sampled frames of the 2026-09-29 recording showed the laptop layout, but VS Code was largely behind Codex and did not provide a numeric zoom reading. Use the actual Code state and saved setting for his connected `-1` / disconnected `1` pair, without claiming the video proves those numbers.

## 2026-09-29 — Keep a linked worktree selected through focus and SCM refresh races

An external `vscode.dev/redirect` Worktree link could silently do nothing when Code took more than two seconds to gain focus. Rapid links also shared a queue with the prior link's Codex focus return, so an older handoff could take Code out of focus before the newer link ran. The link now accepts the latest request in order, skips obsolete requests before their next tree action, waits up to eight seconds for Code to focus, rechecks focus after Git status and SCM settling, and reports a genuine focus timeout. The ordinary mouse staging, navigation, and Undo paths are unchanged.

For an unstaged target, `list.collapseAll` closes resource groups but depends on the active list and can miss peer repository headers. Keep that recursive collapse first, then run VS Code's view-scoped `collapseAllRepositories`, clear the stale tree selection, and reopen the target's change so Auto Reveal expands only that repository. Listen to Git repository state/open/close events during this explicit link and wait for a quiet period after the 500 ms minimum, capped at 2.5 seconds; allow the final reveal to settle before returning focus. This is still a bounded presentation wait, not a private renderer acknowledgement. Staged-only/clean reveals now report their actual success instead of always claiming success. Optional debug logs distinguish an undelivered link from a received, superseded, timed-out, or completed one.

The focused native Mini harness passed first-open, repeat, switch, hidden-SCM, late-staged-refresh, 2.6-second focus handoff, and five rapid links with 24 repositories. It inspected actual virtualized headers across both scroll directions: only the requested repository was expanded, Staged Changes was closed, Changes remained open, and the top unstaged file was selected. Separate native runs passed staged-only, clean, deleted and large staged targets plus two-way and three-way merge-conflict links. The focused real-host Worktree link E2E exited zero on the Mini. The exact AIMVS24 redirect decoded to its requested root and Codex return app in a unit test; all 110 non-GUI tests and lint passed. One Mini E2E attempt without the existing Code executable path downloaded VS Code then failed with EPIPE; the repeat using the installed Code executable passed. The final production JavaScript passed the nine-repository background/rapid-link native run and matched the Mini build at SHA-256 `611222993bc5ea1e44137111587a0f9ca159e88b8e53ac7acf358eaec2c86311`. No MacBook E2E or Extension Development Host was run.

## 2026-09-29 — Show staged Git LFS images in the native preview

VS Code's built-in image preview refuses to show a small `git:` LFS pointer and displays “The image is stored with Git LFS and is not available for preview.” Better Git's staged navigation and a direct click on the Staged Changes row take different paths: navigation opens an image comparison, while the row opens a `TabInputCustom` `imagePreview.previewEditor` with the index `git:` URI. Handle both. For a locally present object, replace the pointer side with a read-only filesystem URI for the exact staged SHA-256 and size, using Git LFS's `LocalMediaDir`. Verify bytes on read; do not smudge, download, change the index, or substitute the working file. If the local object is unavailable, retain VS Code's original behavior.

The focused isolated VS Code 1.138.0 integration test on the Mini passed for navigation and simulated native row opening, including exact staged bytes and unchanged Git status. There was one active image tab and no LFS error or Git-state change. The first click showed an empty editor and the temporary `(Index)` tab title for about 200 ms; repeat clicks took roughly 50–60 ms before the image tab replaced it. Follow-up native checks covered double-clicked persistent tabs, both click/double-click orders, seven rapid clicks, and switching to another file; the image still rendered with a single tab and unchanged Git state. The final focused integration test passed against `db3653f`. Physical MacBook acceptance remains separate; no MacBook E2E or Extension Development Host was run.

## 2026-09-28 — Discover commit models and fall back across CLI accounts

An implicit CLI default did not guarantee the smallest model. Discover candidates through a short-lived, metadata-only Codex app-server `initialize` / `model/list` handshake or Claude Code stream-JSON `initialize`. Refresh per generation, bound discovery time/output/pagination, and kill the discovery process on cancellation or disposal. Do not start a model turn for catalogue lookup or poll in the background. Rank supported numeric generations before family size; do not select hidden, arbitrary default, plan or context-window aliases. Claude's resolved model is more reliable than a display name; older catalogue display names remain a compatibility path.

Codex uses GPT-6 Luna as the documented compatibility baseline and discovers newer recognized small models; Claude chooses the newest advertised generation and then Haiku, Sonnet, Opus or Fable in size order. An older catalogue is not account entitlement: the local standalone Codex 0.145.0 rejected GPT-6 Luna for its ChatGPT account even though the desktop offered Luna. A real synthetic production-method smoke confirmed one Claude fallback, using the local catalogue's Opus 5.5, generated a schema-valid message. Do not claim Luna ran successfully, hard-code the desktop picker as CLI availability, change authentication or silently use a flagship Codex default. A newer Claude CLI may advertise a same-generation smaller model and will then be selected automatically; unfamiliar future names still require a selector update.

Keep the original staged-first bounded context for the entire attempt. A Codex failure tries the installed Claude CLI once; Claude-only remains available and cancellation never means fallback. Both successful and failed runs retain the per-repository reservation, edited-message confirmation and no-auto-commit contract. Progress and Better Git AI output name the selected provider/model without logging diffs, prompts, generated text or raw CLI errors.

Verification: 109 non-GUI tests and lint passed. The isolated Mini hosts passed ten focused commit-message tests on VS Code 1.132.0 and again on 1.138.0 (real registered commands for staged-only, tracked/untracked, Claude-only and forced Codex failure, plus cancellation, disposal and concurrent-request guards). Visible native Mini checks exercised provider selection, model/fallback progress, generation into the intended box and Cancel without starting Claude. On the final 1.138.0 native host, an input edit made after generation began retained its text and displayed the replacement confirmation. Screenshots were inspected. No MacBook Extension Development Host or GUI test was run. Final local and Mini production bundles match at SHA-256 `f1196f8ac88d8821a0f9252ff62ec3db27c334f7b59e3c9d9c492533f088368f`.

## 2026-09-25 — Select diff view by a chosen macOS display

VS Code's Diff View commands only affect an active diff, so an extension must update `diffEditor.renderSideBySide` and `diffEditor.useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited` to establish a mode at startup. Inline uses `renderSideBySide=false`; Automatic uses `renderSideBySide=true` with `useInlineViewWhenSpaceIsLimited=true`. These settings affect VS Code diffs across the application, not only Source Control. Keep the feature off by default through an empty application-scoped display target.

The Dell capture chain currently appears to macOS as `AVT GC553G2`, not as a Dell name. Match its saved CoreGraphics display UUID first, then a unique external display with the configured dimensions if the UUID changes. An ambiguous same-size match or failed probe leaves the current mode alone. A short asynchronous JXA screen-list probe every five seconds while opted in handles unplugging without adding work to any navigation or staging input path. Recheck the configured target after probing to avoid applying a stale target. Do not use `system_profiler` in that polling path.

Verification: 97 non-GUI tests, TypeScript, ESLint and webpack passed. The isolated Mini VS Code host passed connected, disconnected and disabled setting transitions with no diff editor open; the disabled mode retained a manual setting change. The locally built and Mini-tested production JavaScript were byte-identical at SHA-256 `6633ae10071c1357c167f0b4b00a8a823e3174135e782ecbd12a834485331848`. Physical Dell hot-plug on Ethan's MacBook remains untested.

## 2026-09-25 — Pull fresh AgentFlow selections through the editor API

VS Code's Monaco code/diff editors can expose an inaccessible AX text control while screen-reader mode is off. AgentFlow must not enable that mode, synthesize Copy, or treat earlier generic AX tests as actual VS Code coverage. The optional macOS bridge responds over a private same-user Unix socket only to a versioned request matching the focused main PID and a fresh mouse-selection event. It retains coordinates, not selected text; reads at most 8,192 UTF-16 units only on demand; never logs the text; and clears eligibility on document, focus or editor changes. Programmatic Better Git hunk movement is deliberately excluded. No Git navigation debounce, network listener, polling or clipboard mutation is introduced.

Verification: 91 non-GUI tests, TypeScript and ESLint passed. Isolated Mini VS Code 1.132.0 and 1.138.0 workbenches, with screen-reader mode off, passed mouse selections in plain and diff editors through the production bridge and AgentFlow's production Swift socket reader. Malformed/wrong-PID/stale requests and programmatic selections returned null; original fixture files stayed unchanged. Terminal/webview, remote and multi-cursor selection support is not claimed.

Save a compact text-view snapshot in the same three-entry transaction receipt before Better Git stages. The observer captures the still-visible text view for VS Code's own Git stage, and tagged mouse navigation retains its original view when it crosses files before staging. For a held batch, the live previewed endpoint takes priority over the earlier mouse origin. Restore the selection and top visible line only after the guarded index Undo succeeds and the affected working-tree view opens; binary/deleted files and older receipts retain the previous fallback. Keep presentation outside the history lock.

These restore the exact selection and viewport after Better Git Stage + Next, built-in Git Stage, a held mouse stage and a late source-tagged mouse stage; a held batch returns to its last previewed file. ## 2026-09-22 — Reveal worktrees with no unstaged files without opening an editor

The old link picked a staged file when no working changes existed, and skipped expansion entirely when clean. Native `workbench.scm.action.focusNextInput` can select a repository through its commit input; the Git API `repository.ui.selected` and `onDidChange` identify that exact repository even while the editor remains in another worktree. Use one bounded pass through native inputs, with one temporary listener per repository, rather than file previews or generic row traversal. For staged-only targets, focus its resource group, recursively collapse once, clear selection, then reopen the same now-hidden input. Completely clean targets need only repository collapse followed by that input reveal. No editor/tab, index, file, workspace-folder or user-setting mutation is needed.

The resource-group focus command returns before its queued tree work finishes. The explicit link retains the existing 500 ms Git-to-SCM resource allowance and adds a bounded 100 ms group-focus settle; these are presentation margins, not renderer acknowledgements. The input pass has a two-second safety bound and aborts on window/editor changes. Auto Reveal off or hidden commit inputs skip this route. Startup and the ordinary manual collapse command retain their independent contracts.

Verification: 89 non-GUI tests, TypeScript, ESLint and packaging passed. The Mini VS Code 1.132.0 suite passed 32 Worktree-link integration cases; native visible tests passed eight no-unstaged cases plus six existing dirty-worktree cases, including repeat, clean, staged deletions, large staged groups and newly discovered staged/clean worktrees via the real URI handler with Graph initially focused. Native checks assert only the exact repository expanded, Staged Changes collapsed, no selected file, no transient editor/tab change and unchanged Git status. Screenshots were inspected. Final production bundle SHA-256: `8101c1fffa6b2d3c8a030ef2788e2e598a84f5cf51b91a3ec37e92fa22455c7f`.

## 2026-09-20 — Wait for VS Code focus before Chrome-originated SCM collapse

A Worktree link delivered while the receiving Code window was behind Chrome could run Source Control commands against a partially rendered tree. The Mini reproduced the failure when several repositories were expanded: the link completed with only two visible repository headers, so the target tree did not reliably collapse peers. The collapse command and existing 500 ms SCM resource-settle allowance were correct; the missing precondition was native window focus.

The link reveal now waits on `window.onDidChangeWindowState` until the receiving VS Code window is focused, with a two-second safety bound. It then runs the existing exact-target reveal, recursive `list.collapseAll`, and selection clear. No polling, recollapse loop, arbitrary input debounce, renderer connection, or staging/mouse behavior changed. If Code never becomes focused, the reveal is skipped rather than acting on the wrong tree.

Verification: 89 non-GUI tests, lint and packaging passed. The Mini background-link harness passed with Code focused after 0 ms, 50 ms, and 1.2 seconds while Finder was frontmost initially; each case left only the requested repository expanded, Staged Changes collapsed, the Changes group open, and the top unstaged file selected. The test used unmodified VS Code 1.138.0 and retained the existing Graph/repeat/switch checks.

## 2026-09-20 — Keep the origin next in native app switching

The optional application-scoped `worktreeLinkKeepEditorFront` setting activates the configured/link origin after worktree reveal, waits for native activation acknowledgement, then reactivates the exact captured VS Code process. It requires the existing return-focus setting and defaults off. Existing links and configurable origins remain unchanged; no app launches or mouse/staging changes are involved.

NSWorkspace caches frontmostApplication within a JXA process. Sleeping the script thread left it stale and the first native test finished in the origin instead of Code. Pumping NSRunLoop during the bounded acknowledgement wait fixed this; native activation notifications prove Code → origin → the same Code PID. Abort if another app intervenes, the origin is missing/ambiguous, or the editor cannot be identified safely.

## 2026-09-19 — Editor clicks must not cancel a physical stage hold

Mouse/keyboard cursor changes, manual tab changes and edits previously called the same invalidation routine as explicit cancellation. The native Mini reproduction on 1.2.92 lost its ready badges after an editor click. Preserve the active source-owned hold and captured selection across these UI changes while still invalidating queued navigation. A released ready selection becomes a captured Git transaction: subsequent editor changes may suppress its follow-up navigation, never silently discard its stage. Keep repository/live-path validation and explicit cancellation; remove the arbitrary 60-second expiry for physical holds. Do not close an unrelated active editor when staging captured files.

Verification: 87 non-GUI tests and 30 focused VS Code integration tests passed. The latter cover both mouse sources, edits and manual editor switches, a hold exceeding 60 seconds, a simultaneous release/editor-switch race, short/long release, explicit cancellation and exact batch Undo. Native Mini editor and gutter clicks retained ready badges; further wheel selection, release and one batch Undo passed (`BETTER_GIT_HELD_EDITOR_CLICK_VERIFIED`). Tested production JavaScript SHA-256: `26587961930d374457b0f3bb305b38c50b847192e25f95efa6170ab9fffe4e6c`. Physical mouse acceptance remains separate.

Agentic Mouse coordination confirmed 1.0.221 sends release on physical key-up and has no editor-click cancellation path. Its intentionally disabled stageRangePreviewNavigation feature remains disabled; no mouse-side update is needed for this fix.

Release: PR #162 merged as `57505d2`. Version 1.2.93 passed `BETTER_GIT_MARKETPLACE_RELEASE_VERIFIED` with exact VSIX SHA-256 `3ed31b3a09f2d0aa2af45fee475c2a31d433fc5444e16bb91b5aa71e7950d354`. Gallery installation retained the Marketplace UUID, no version pin and effective automatic updates; installed JavaScript matches the tested bundle. Extension Host activation and physical mouse acceptance remain pending.

## 2026-09-18 — Preview navigation must remain inside the source-owned stage hold

Agentic Mouse can temporarily repurpose the wheel while a stage-ready range remains selected. Accept that navigation only for the exact source whose hold is active, ready, and still owns a nonempty selection. Route every step through the existing serialized next/previous diff navigation so rapid ratchets cannot reorder editor state. Preview navigation must not clear the ready decoration, rebuild the range, stage anything, or change the later release transaction; releasing the original hold still stages the preserved batch and advances normally.

The mouse-side adjacent cell is only a momentary modifier during this chord. Better Git therefore exposes a private source-tagged URI for navigation rather than changing the public Stage-and-Next command or inferring physical button state inside VS Code.

The focused real-host test passed on the Mac mini against VS Code 1.138.0. It exercised next and previous preview navigation, retained the two-file selection and ready badges, then released the hold and verified that exactly the preserved batch staged before normal advancement.

Better Git 1.2.92 passed the required Marketplace gate with SHA-256 `532fd728866ce8397841f76214a8068692c27096a7a922051a58b73ff9af6994`. The gallery install selected 1.2.92 with `metadata.source=gallery`; its production bundle hash matched the packaged source, and the restarted primary extension host activated Better Git after the update.

## Avoid activating background staged editors during review navigation (2026-09-17)

**Trigger:** Staging a file sometimes expanded Staged Changes and jumped Source Control, especially with several pinned review tabs already open.

**Cause:** `openNavigationTarget` closed the active editor before opening its destination. Closing a pinned tab briefly activated a background staged-only `file:` editor. VS Code's native `scm.autoReveal` then expanded Staged Changes and scrolled to that resource; opening the next unstaged diff did not restore the collapsed group. The isolated native reproduction used F18 without Agentic Mouse and failed on 1.2.90, so the demonstrated cause is Better Git's tab handoff rather than the mouse transport.

**Change:** Open the destination first, then close only the captured, now-inactive clean original tab with the public tab API and `preserveFocus`. Keep the original if the destination failed to open, the original became dirty, or it is still active. No delay, mouse timing change, automatic recollapse or global SCM setting change is needed; deliberately expanded groups remain expanded.

**Verification:** All 87 non-GUI tests, compilation, lint and production packaging passed. Twelve focused real VS Code 1.138.0 E2Es on the Mini passed pinned Next/Previous without transient background activation, rapid staging, range preview/cancel, batch staging, exact Undo and its three-entry cap. The native `--stage-reveal` scenario reproduced the expanded group on the old implementation and passed the fix across Next, Previous, repeated stage, three Undos, pinned batch release/Undo and preservation of a deliberately expanded group, alongside existing worktree switch checks. Inspected screenshots show Staged Changes collapsed with the correct next unstaged file selected. The tested production JavaScript is SHA-256 `c376a66bc3fc7d9003d0a353d4c64b8b420ca5104459525119b5ce11467b20de`. Physical mouse acceptance remains separate from these native command tests.

**Release:** PR #160 merged as `15e64f7`. Version 1.2.91 passed the required verifier with `BETTER_GIT_MARKETPLACE_RELEASE_VERIFIED identity=EthanSK.better-git-vscode version=1.2.91 sha256=14db58cbcc9d31f42f3f88227c1ecf8f3c8ced6d3898ada54884ded742df0b42`. The initial uploads hit socket hang-ups; the same VSIX was eventually uploaded through the existing task-scoped Mini CONNECT proxy and passed validation. Ethan's installation was updated through the Marketplace identifier; its production JavaScript matches the tested bundle above, with Gallery association, no version pin and automatic updates retained. The Extension Host was deliberately not restarted; activation remains pending **Developer: Restart Extension Host**.

## Preview every endpoint of a held mouse stage range (2026-09-16)

**Trigger:** Ethan wanted every wheel detent during a stage-ready mouse hold to open the newest file added to the pending batch in the main editor. Cancelling the batch needed to return to the exact diff view from before range selection.

**Cause:** The range state previously changed only Source Control decorations. Opening endpoint previews also exposed two asynchronous VS Code races: Git can replace a preview tab after its command promise resolves, and the review-decoration refresh can briefly resolve the old file or no file while the replacement tab renders. Either event could invalidate or clear the live selection before the next wheel detent.

**Change:** Version 1.2.90 opens the range cursor after every effective wheel move through the existing serialized navigation queue. Every held gesture captures its original tab input, diff resources, text selections and viewport. The adjacent stage-ready cancel chord restores that exact view and consumes the later release. Delayed Git tab replacement is accepted only for the source-owned preview target, while manual tab changes still invalidate the hold; transient decoration refreshes no longer own cancellation. Multi-file release keeps the existing batch stage, advance target and one-transaction Undo behavior.

**Verification:** TypeScript compilation, lint, production packaging and all 87 non-GUI tests passed. A focused isolated VS Code 1.138.0 run on the Mini passed endpoint preview after each wheel detent, reverse contraction, upward range growth, wrong-source rejection, batch stage and advance, one exact Undo, and cancellation restoring the original tracked diff, cursor selection and viewport while making the later release inert. The Mini and packaged local production JavaScript match at SHA-256 `5872fac2cff2fb6e239a009962d58795397827ef3fa9f6fd9df1db30fd30cc9b`.

**Release:** PR #159 merged as `54dfe57`. Version 1.2.90 passed the required verifier with `BETTER_GIT_MARKETPLACE_RELEASE_VERIFIED identity=EthanSK.better-git-vscode version=1.2.90 sha256=37a35fb4ffb155a906a629074983b0f58e0367eeb5908a2408eaed089776d2a3`. ## Treat a held keyboard stage shortcut as one physical gesture (2026-09-16)

**Trigger:** Holding a Shift+Option Stage-and-Next/Previous keyboard shortcut let macOS key repeat invoke the VS Code command again and again, staging and advancing through several files before the key was released. Ethan required one stage per continuous hold and another stage only after release and a fresh press.

**Cause:** VS Code re-dispatches contributed keybindings for repeated key-down events, while `registerCommand` receives no key-up event. The Stage-and-Next/Previous handlers therefore could not distinguish one physical hold from rapid separate presses. Applying a generic debounce would also slow legitimate mouse F18/F19 staging and still would not identify the real release boundary.

**Change:** Version 1.2.89 tags only the eight contributed QWERTY/Dvorak keyboard bindings with their physical key identity. A keyboard-only guard admits the first invocation and suppresses further tagged invocations until a persistent, shell-free `/usr/bin/osascript` JXA monitor observes that physical macOS key released through `CGEventSourceKeyState`. Physical X/Z and comma/period share key codes across the two layouts. Exact release has no debounce or elapsed-time guess; monitor failure uses a 500 ms quiet-period fallback that repeated key-down events continuously extend. Untagged command-palette, status-bar and mouse F18/F19 calls bypass the guard completely.

**Verification:** TypeScript compilation, lint, production packaging and all 87 non-GUI tests passed. The release-monitor JXA ran successfully against the live macOS CoreGraphics API, and its process protocol, allowlists, chunking, failures, timeout and disposal passed dedicated tests. A focused real VS Code 1.132.0 run on the Mini passed the QWERTY/Dvorak manifest mappings. A second isolated native-workbench run sent 25 repeated Shift+Option+Q key-downs for the Mini's Dvorak physical X binding and proved exactly one stage, then proved release/re-press staged the next file and untagged F18 still staged independently. The Mini and local production JavaScript match at SHA-256 `43bb9ba2dfb09f35549779cfe12bfcaeb0b1e10606fd84d031e4e881f469593a`. The Mini's SSH process lacks macOS Input Monitoring (`CGPreflightPostEventAccess=false`), so the burst exercised VS Code's real repeated-keydown dispatch while the actual CoreGraphics release query was verified separately; final physical keyboard acceptance remains after installation and Extension Host restart.

**Independent implementation review:** Ethan requested Fable. Canonical `claude-fable-5-1` produced the first implementation pass. Codex retained its keyboard-only manifest tagging, allowlisted persistent JXA monitor and safe fallback, then removed Fable's 30 ms release plausibility delay and two-second exact-mode idle expiry because both could swallow a valid fast re-press or turn an unreleased key into a new stage. The finished exact path ends only on the physical release signal.

**Release:** PR #158 merged as `e9dc211`. Version 1.2.89 passed the required verifier with `BETTER_GIT_MARKETPLACE_RELEASE_VERIFIED identity=EthanSK.better-git-vscode version=1.2.89 sha256=8f9ec53818e7706c6f4d7188d82d024f0d37c96f3208f8981e5a593166cde3fe`. The first direct upload stalled with a socket hang-up, so the unchanged VSIX was uploaded and verified through a task-scoped CONNECT proxy backed by the Mini; no persistent network setting changed. Ethan's installation was updated through the Marketplace identifier and retained `source=gallery`, Marketplace UUID `939b51df-f995-4799-88fa-ae47815cabb2`, publisher UUID `78eae69f-3d8c-4060-a72c-ca4862edb593`, `pinned=false` and default automatic updates. Its installed production JavaScript matches the tested release bundle at SHA-256 `43bb9ba2dfb09f35549779cfe12bfcaeb0b1e10606fd84d031e4e881f469593a`. The Extension Host was deliberately not restarted, so activation and final physical keyboard confirmation remain pending.

## Match adjacent Undo to the live bare-F16 transport (2026-09-16)

**Trigger:** After 1.2.87 was installed and activated, Ethan confirmed ordinary Better Git Undo still worked but the quick adjacent physical chord did not. That split isolated the remaining defect to the active-hold input route rather than Git restoration or saved Undo history.

**Cause:** The live Agentic Mouse Karabiner rules emit source-tagged F14, **bare F16**, then source-tagged F15 for all four source/direction paths. Version 1.2.87 tested and fixed only the source-tagged F16 binding, so the bare F16 used by the live user keybinding still saw an active hold as cancel-only. A second edge existed when button-down captured no unstaged review item: queued F14 work could tear the hold down before F16 decided it.

**Change:** Version 1.2.88 gives bare and source-tagged F16 the same synchronous input-order rule. Before readiness, F16 cancels the unfinished hold and performs the exact previous staging Undo; after readiness, it cancels only the pending stage and preserves Undo history. Release-only holds now survive F14 until F16 or the F15 boundary decides them even without a captured review item. If readiness cannot produce a stage-ready decoration, the chord returns to the pre-ready Undo meaning. Short navigation, long staging, wheel range selection, experimental button-down behavior and the three-entry cap retain their existing paths.

**Verification:** All 58 non-GUI tests, TypeScript compilation, lint and the production build passed. The exact production JavaScript passed 45 focused real VS Code 1.132.0 cases on the Mac Mini: live tagged-F14/bare-F16/tagged-F15 sequences and tagged fallback sequences for both mice and directions; pre-ready Undo; ready-state cancellation; missing review items; inert later releases; short and long holds; wheel-range staging; experimental button-down holds; rapid Undo; and the three-entry cap. The Mini, packaged VSIX and installed Gallery extension share JavaScript SHA-256 `815bb08ed9f84746e3f10f1bfb8304d6bdc3df5a30a819a5981604f7d1ef85f2`.

**Release:** PR #156 merged as `7905060`. Version 1.2.88 passed the required verifier with `BETTER_GIT_MARKETPLACE_RELEASE_VERIFIED identity=EthanSK.better-git-vscode version=1.2.88 sha256=d017ad17a71444e1a19a3b20ccbb2c35a970a57ae91856b80589448e88e0f995`. ## Restore pre-ready adjacent Undo without changing ready-hold cancellation (2026-09-16)

**Trigger:** Ethan clarified the two adjacent-button outcomes. Pressing the adjacent cell before a Next/Previous hold reaches the 200 ms stage-ready threshold must cancel the unfinished hold and Undo the previous completed stage. Pressing it after the held row is stage-ready must cancel only that pending stage and preserve the existing Undo history.

**Cause:** Version 1.2.85 made source-tagged F16 an atomic hold-cancel command, but treated every active hold as cancel-only. That removed the earlier pre-ready Undo outcome. Deciding from queued editor work would also be racy: Agentic Mouse's transaction arrives as source-tagged F14, F16 and F15, and the readiness command can be waiting behind other navigation work even though its physical input already arrived.

**Change:** Version 1.2.87 records `stageReadyRequested` synchronously when the source-owned readiness command arrives. A source-tagged F16 always cancels the active hold; before readiness it then runs the existing exact staging Undo, while after readiness it stops after cancellation. The later F15 boundary remains harmless. Normal short-release navigation, long-release staging, wheel range selection, stale-event rejection and the three-entry global Undo cap are unchanged.

**Verification:** TypeScript compilation, lint, production packaging and all 52 non-GUI tests passed. A focused real VS Code run on the Mini passed eight cases covering both mouse sources and both directions: pre-ready adjacent cancellation performed exact Undo, while stage-ready adjacent cancellation preserved the earlier stage receipt.
